國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和訊號 
經濟 
⚫ S&P Global 公布，9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數(PMI Composite Output 
Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4，創逾 5 年新高；服務業及製造業
PMI 分別升至 58.7 及 57.0。在內需強勁帶動下，新訂單與就業同步成
長，惟供應鏈瓶頸及能源成本攀升推升通膨壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國新屋銷售年化率為 68.4 萬戶，月增 6.4%、年減 2.0%，顯示銷
售較前月回升但需求尚未完全恢復。中西部與南部為主要支撐，惟房價
年減、庫存仍維持 48 萬戶以上，供給相對充裕，後續銷售能否延續仍
待觀察。 
⚫ 9 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.1，月減 7.0%、年減 12.7%，創近四個
月低點。當前狀況與預期指數同步下滑，反映高物價、燃料價格與貿易
不確定性持續壓抑信心；同時通膨預期升溫，後續消費動能仍待觀察。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 中國國家主席習近平於 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪美並與川普舉行峰會 ， 雙方
同意延長貿易休兵至 2027 年 1 月 10 日，推動部分商品降稅、建立 AI
對話機制及持續磋商稀土供應議題，並延續高層溝通。台灣議題未列入
成果清單，關稅、科技競爭及供應鏈等核心分歧仍未化解，後續談判發
展仍待觀察。 
短評 
⚫ 美國 9 月 PMI 強勁擴張，顯示企業活動與就業動能仍具韌性，但投入
成本與售價上升也反映通膨壓力升溫。相較之下，房市需求尚未全面恢
復，消費信心則明顯轉弱，顯示內需前景仍有隱憂；美中延長貿易休兵
雖有助穩定市場，但核心分歧未解，後續不確定性仍高。 
歐 
元 
區 
9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
經濟 ⚫ 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，服務業 PMI 增1.4 點至 53，製造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7。 
短評 
⚫ 儘管 9 月經濟表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲
央行的鷹派態度，使得 10 月升息一碼的可能性些微增加，亦強化未來
升息預期。

中 
國 
美中貿易延長休戰，惟後續協商成效仍待觀察 
經濟 ⚫ 1-8 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.6%、17.6%。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 習近平赴華府與美國總統川普舉行峰會，雙方同意貿易休戰期延長至
2027 年 1 月 10 日，並成立雙邊貿易理事會，但在科技管制、稀土供應
及關稅等核心議題上，仍未取得突破。 
⚫ 美中雙方就「300 億美元對 300 億美元」對等降稅架構達成共識，包
含中國製造的小型家電、玩具、節日裝飾品及兒童汽車安全座椅等，以
及美國生產的農產品、海鮮、原木、化妝品及醫療器材等。超過90%的
清單內產品將免除雙方此前相互加徵的關稅，並適用最惠國關稅待遇。 
短評 
⚫ 美中雙方貿易休戰期只延長兩個月，反映美國仍保留談判槓桿，持續給
予中國稀土供應履約的壓力。 
⚫ 美中每年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程
設備、電動車、軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，本次降稅實質效益有限。 
東 
(南) 
亞 
亞銀上修亞洲及太平洋經濟體 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.7% 
經濟 
⚫ 亞銀預估 2026 年印度 GDP 為 7.0%，通膨率為 5.0%。 
⚫ 亞銀預估 2026 年東協國家 GDP 為 4.7%，其中越南 GDP 達 7.8%表現
最佳。 
重要 
事件 ⚫ 印尼央行 9 月會議第三度維持基準利率於 5.75%不變。 
短評 ⚫ AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨與信用風險的重要變數。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.9.29 
一、 美國—PMI 大幅走升、新屋銷售回穩，消費信心薄弱，美中關係暫現緩和
訊號 
標普全球 (S&P Global) 公布， 2026 年 9 月美國採購經理人綜合指數 (PMI 
Composite Output Index)初值由上月的 56.0 升至 58.4， 連續第四個月攀升 ， 創2021
年 7 月以來新高；其中服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI)由 56.5 升至 58.7，創
59 個月高點，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 由 53.9 升至 57.0，創
2022 年 5 月以來最高水準，顯示美國企業活動持續強勁擴張。受國內市場需求穩
健帶動，製造業與服務業新訂單同步加速成長，推升整體產出增速升至逾 5 年來
最快水準，企業亦積極擴增人力，就業成長速度創 2022 年 6 月以來新高。另一
方面，積壓訂單增加及供應鏈延遲加劇，反映產能利用率持續攀升；加以近期油
價上漲推升燃料及運輸成本，使企業投入成本增幅升至 2022 年 10 月以來最高，
帶動售價漲幅回升，顯示通膨壓力有所升溫。儘管如此，企業對未來一年營運前
景仍維持正向看法，反映市場對美國經濟韌性及需求持續擴張仍具信心。 
2026 年 8 月美國新屋銷售經季節性調整年化率為 68.4 萬戶 ， 較上月增加6.4%，
但較去年同期下降 2.0%，顯示新屋銷售較前月回升，惟仍低於去年同期水準。地
區別表現分歧，其中中西部及南部分別較上月增加 84.9%及 6.9%，東北部及西部
則分別減少 36.1%及 15.2%。價格方面，8 月新屋銷售中位價為 39.37 萬美元，較
上月小升 0.4%，但較去年同期下降 5.8%。供給方面，8 月底經季節性調整後待售
新屋數量為 48.3 萬戶，與上月持平，較去年同期減少 2.0%；按目前銷售速度估
算，供給月數為 8.5 個月，低於上月的 9.0 個月，與去年同期大致持平。整體而
言，8 月美國新屋銷售較前月回升，主要受中西部與南部銷售增加帶動，惟仍低
於去年同期，顯示需求動能尚未完全恢復。價格方面，中位價較去年同期下滑，
反映市場價格仍有調整壓力；同時待售新屋維持在 48 萬戶以上，供給月數仍達
8.5 個月，顯示整體供給相對充裕，後續仍須觀察銷售回升是否具持續性。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 9 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.1，低於上月
的 51.7， 月減7.0%、 年減12.7%， 為近四個月低點 ， 並較2026 年 1 月下降約 15%，
顯示消費者信心持續轉弱。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由51.9 降至 50.9，月減

1.9%、 年減15.7%； 消費者預期指數則由51.5 降至 46.3， 月減10.1%、 年減10.4%，
反映民眾對未來景氣與個人財務前景的看法明顯惡化。調查指出，消費者對高物
價的擔憂持續升高，當前與未來一年個人財務看法均較上月轉弱約一成；短期企
業展望也因燃料價格偏高與貿易爭端再度升溫而明顯下滑。政治立場方面，各陣
營受訪者普遍認為年初以來經濟前景轉弱，其中共和黨人信心較 1 月下降 20%，
民主黨人下降 13%。 通膨預期方面 ， 未來一年通膨預期由8 月的 4.0%升至 4.6%，
為今年 6 月以來最高，且高於今年 2 月的 3.4%(美伊爆發衝突之前)及 2024 年各
月水準；長期通膨預期亦由 3.3%升至 3.4%，結束連續三個月持平，並高於 2024
年 2.8%至 3.2%的區間。整體而言，9 月美國消費信心再度降溫，顯示高物價、燃
料價格與貿易不確定性持續壓抑民眾信心，後續仍須觀察通膨預期升溫是否進一
步影響消費動能。 
中國國家主席習近平於 2026 年 9 月 23 日至 25 日訪問美國，並於 9 月 24 日
在華府白宮與美國總統川普舉行峰會。會後，美中雙方未發布聯合聲明，而是分
別公布成果清單，顯示雙方在穩定雙邊關係及維持高層溝通方面取得部分共識，
包括同意建構「基於尊重、公平、對等的建設性戰略穩定關係」、推動 300 億美
元非敏感商品對等降稅安排、建立人工智慧 (AI)或「超級智慧」對話與風險溝通
機制，以及持續就稀土與關鍵礦產供應鏈議題進行磋商。此外，美方並表示中國
將於 2027 年及 2028 年分別進口至少 1,000 萬公噸美國煤炭。值得注意的是，台
灣議題並未出現在雙方公布的成果文件中；另一方面，中方則額外強調兩軍危機
溝通機制及合作搜尋失蹤美軍遺骸等事項。整體而言，本次川習會有助延續美中
對話與降低短期緊張情勢，惟雙方在關稅、科技競爭、供應鏈安全及其他戰略議
題上的結構性分歧仍未完全化解，後續談判進展及雙邊互動方向仍有待持續觀察。 
二、歐元區—9 月景氣增強，進一步推高升息預期 
 歐元區 9 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)增 1.1 點至 53.1，且為連續四個月上
升。其中服務業PMI 增 1.4 點至 53，創近 10 個月高點，主要反映 AI 與國防投資
活動增加，帶動商業服務需求，抵銷能源價格上漲對消費購買力的負面影響。製
造業 PMI 則持平於 52.7，不過，德國萊茵河水位下降，沿岸較為發達的化工、製
藥業物流成本增加，因而暫時壓抑製造業上行空間。值得注意的是，由於國際原
油價格回升，綜合投入、產出物價指數皆上升至近 4 個月最高。故儘管 9 月經濟
表現優於預期，但通膨壓力也同步升溫，再加上先前歐洲央行的鷹派態度，使得

三、日本—9 月綜合 PMI 維持擴張，製造業表現仍具韌性 
 日本 9 月綜合 PMI 由 53.5 降至 52.5，製造業與服務業同步回落，經濟成長動
能較前兩個月的高檔有所降溫。製造業 PMI 下跌 0.8 點至 54.1，雖然產出、新訂
單及出口訂單增速放緩，但外部需求仍相當強勁，尤其 AI 相關需求可能持續帶
動出口表現，加上企業開始重建庫存、供應鏈瓶頸尚未完全解除，預料補庫存需
求將在未來數月繼續支撐生產成長。服務業 PMI 下降 0.9 點至 51.6，受到工資、
運輸及能源成本上升影響 ， 需求動能略有降溫 ， 但積壓訂單與出口業務有所改善 。
另，投入成本與售價漲幅均較前期放緩，顯示通膨壓力正逐步緩解，但整體物價
水準仍維持高檔。此外，企業對未來景氣的信心持續改善，未來活動指數升至七
個月高點，反映企業普遍預期經濟擴張趨勢仍可望延續。 
四、中國—美中貿易延長休戰，惟後續協商成效仍待觀察 
1-8 月工業部門營業收入、利潤年增率分別為 6.6%、17.6%，其中營業收入年
增率較上期(1-7 月)略增 0.1 個百分點，但利潤年增率下降 1.9 個百分點。受惠於
金屬、塑化原物料上漲，加上 AI 需求擴張，以及政府推動高科技產業發展等因
素，營收成長貢獻度較高者包含有色金屬加工(營收、利潤年增 31.1%、71.8%)、
計算機、通信和其他電子設備製造業(營收、利潤年增20.1%、109.9%)、化學原料
及製品(營收、利潤年增 9.1%、51%)。此外，電氣機械及器材業、汽車業營收年
增率雖分別為 11.5%、2.9%，但獲利分別年減 5.2%、16%。非金屬礦物製品業營
收、獲利下滑 9.5、46.7%，黑色金屬加工業營收、獲利分別年減 1.1%、62.4%，
反映產業持續進行減產所致。整體而言，部分產業受惠於短期原物料價格波動，
以及受到政策推動的高科技產業表現較佳，惟部分產業因陷入產能調整週期，或
政府減少支持等因素而表現較弱，產業分化明顯。 
展望未來，儘管美中雙方互相降低等值 300 億美元產品關稅，惟考量雙方每
年貿易總額逾 4,000 億美元，且降稅產品並非半導體、先進製程設備、電動車、
軍民兩用技術等關鍵領域，因此實質效益有限。另外，雖然美中雙方同意延長貿
易休戰，但休戰期只延長兩個月，反映美國仍保留談判槓桿，持續給予中國稀土
供應履約的壓力。後續將關注美中能否在休戰期間就開放市場、科技競爭、供應

用相關內容。

鏈安全等議題取得進一步共識，若談判不如預期，美國仍可能重新祭出新的反制
措施 ， 而中國亦可能運用稀土作為談判籌碼 ， 經貿關係不確定性仍是較高 。 此外 ，
預計 10 月 8 日舉行歐中貿易會談，若談判未能取得突破，歐盟對中國的貿易限
制恐進一步升級，對中國出口形成額外壓力。 
五、印度及東南亞—亞銀上修亞洲及太平洋經濟體 2026 年 GDP 預估至 4.7% 
亞 洲 開 發 銀 行 (ADB) 最 新 經 濟 展 望 (Asian Development Outlook, ADO 
September 2026 )上修 2026 年亞洲及太平洋地區經濟體 2026 年經濟成長率(GDP)
至 4.7%(7 月預估 4.5%)、2027 年為 4.5%。ADB 認為，亞洲開發中經濟體雖面臨
中東衝突延長、俄烏戰爭再升溫、能源價格高檔震盪及強烈聖嬰現象等逆風，但
在政府刺激政策、民間投資及 AI 帶動科技出口支撐下，區域經濟仍展現韌性。
通膨方面，2026 年通膨率下修至 3.9%，惟能源與糧食價格壓力仍高，2027 年通
膨預測上修至 3.3%。 
區域經濟表現方面 ， 南亞成長最強勁 ，2026 年 GDP 成長率預估上修至 6.4%，
主要受印度內需與投資帶動；東亞則受中國內需疲弱影響，成長維持平穩；東協
國家(ASEAN)上調至 4.7%， 其中越南 、 馬來西亞及泰國受惠於科技與製造業投資
而獲上修。中亞及西亞、太平洋地區則面臨能源及食品價格上漲壓力，成長預測
略為下修至 3.7%、3.0%。(詳下表) 
ADB 認為未來最大風險包括地緣政治衝突升級、聖嬰 現象造成糧食供應衝
擊，以及 AI 相關資產可能出現價格重估。整體而言，AI 驅動的科技投資仍是亞
洲經濟體最重要的成長動能來源，而能源、糧食及氣候風險將成為影響各國通膨
與信用風險的重要變數。

用相關內容。

五、台灣—AI 浪潮推升接單動能，外銷訂單再創新高 
8 月外銷訂單達 1,029.6 億美元，首度突破千億美元大關，其中受惠於 ASIC
晶片需求帶動，資通產品接單翻倍、電子產品接單亦大增 83.9%，惟光學器材因
面板需求維持低檔，年減 5%。非科技產品方面，基本金屬年增 26.8%，係因銅箔
及銅箔基板受惠 AI 相關供應鏈需求及銅價續漲，加以鍍鋅鋼需求回溫；機械年
增 28.3%，係因半導體業者增加資本支出，帶動半導體設備、自動化設備需求延
續；化學品年增 15.4%，主因藥品接單成長，加上產業用化學品需求持續增加；
塑橡膠製品年增 6.7%， 主係塑膠原料受原料上漲而推升產品售價 ， 加上部分塑膠
製品訂單回溫。展望未來，經濟部統計處預估 2026 年外銷訂單有望突破 1 兆美
元，在 AI 相關需求持續擴張帶動下，科技產品表現可望維持強勁，而傳統產業
中下游產品亦可望受惠於相關供應鏈需求，惟上游原物料價格競爭激烈，後續仍
須關注第四季消費旺季是否有相關備貨需求。

Document 063: 2026年9月财政数据快评：收入向下，力度向上

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月财政数据快评：收入向下，力度向上.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-10-25
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
前三季度，全国一般公共预算收入约 17.2万亿元，同比增长 5.1%。其中，全国税收收入约 14.1万亿元，
同比增长 6.1%。全国一般公共预算支出约 21.0万亿元，同比增长 1.0%。
评论：
9 月财政收入增速下滑，但政策力度指数继续回升。
第一本账收入转负，支出基本持平。9月收入同比下降 0.1%，较前值（4.7%）回落转负；税收收入中增值
税、个人所得税为主要拉动，消费税仍为最大单项拖累，企业所得税、证券交易印花税转负。支出当月同
比 0.03%，基本持平。后续随着基数抬高，收入将延续回落，支出端则预计逐渐向着预算增速追赶。
第二本账收入降幅再度扩大，支出降幅收窄。9 月第二本账收入同比-15.9%，土地出让收入同比-23.2%，
降幅均再度扩大，后续随着地产新政落地可能难有较大回升；支出同比下降 12.3%，降幅明显收窄，主要
是土地以外的其他支出降幅明显收窄，反映债务资金拨付有所加快。
总体来看，广义财政收支增速开始相向而行，力度指数连续回升。9月广义收入当月同比转负、广义支出

降幅明显收窄，二者增速差显著收窄，广义财政缺口同比收缩幅度也有所放缓。四季度只要增速达到 4.2%
以上，全年经济增速就能完成 4.5%的目标，5500 亿债务限额下达后，再出台增量政策的必要性并不算太
强。
风险提示：海外市场动荡，存在不确定性。
 一般公共预算：收入转负、支出持平
2026年10月24日

### Page 2

9 月财政收入小幅转负。1-9 月财政收入累计同比 5.1%，前值（1-8月）5.7%，有所回落。其中税收收入
累计 6.1%，前值 6.6%。单月来看，一般公共预算收入当月同比-0.1%，前值 4.7%，小幅转负。税收收入当
月同比 0.2%，前值 5.6%，连续第二个月回落；非税收入当月同比-1.0%，前值 0.3%，再度转负。税收显著
回落，主要是去年基数继续抬高叠加企业所得税等主体税种走弱；非税收入短暂转正后再度小幅负增长，
收入端整体进入趋势性回落通道，预计后续可能会进一步回落，逐渐靠近年初预算的收入目标（2.2%）。

主要税种增速分化，增值税回升、消费税仍为最大拖累。主要税种中增值税同比 7.3%，较前值（4.0%）回升，

拉动税收收入 3.1个百分点、为最大拉动项；个人所得税同比 10.5%，较前值（11.8%）小幅回落，拉动税
收 1.1个百分点，累计同比 14.1%，仍在高速增长区间；企业所得税同比-0.4%，较前值（11.6%）明显回
落转负；消费税同比-8.5%，降幅较前值（-14.0%）收窄，拖累税收 1.0个百分点、仍为最大的单项拖累。
房地产相关税种整体降幅由-1.9%明显扩大至-14.6%，其中契税（-16.9%）仍然疲弱，土地增值税（-22.2%）
再度转负。此外，证券交易印花税同比-11.5%（前值 17.9%），在 2025 年 9 月同比 342%的超高基数上转
负，拖累税收 0.3 个百分点，后续可能随成交量走弱持续降低；进口环节增值税和消费税同比 12.9%，拉
动税收 1.9个百分点，与增速较高的进口数据较为一致。

### Page 3

图5：各税种对税收收入的拉动排序 图6：主要税种同比增速
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
支出端与上月基本持平。一般公共支出当月同比 0.03%，较前值（-0.1%）小幅回升。节奏上，一般公共预
算支出当月完成 9.6%，与去年同期（9.7%）基本一致。累计来看，1-9月第一本账支出增速为 1.0%，累计
进度 70.0%，与去年同期（70.1%）大体相当；但离 4.4%的预算支出增速水平差距仍然较大，后续有待存
量资金加快拨付带动支出增速回升，全年增速预计回升到 2-3%之间。
结构上，民生支出转负、基建降幅收窄。民生类支出同比增速-0.9%，较前值（1.9%）回落转负。其中，
教育支出同比增长 3.5%，前值 1.3%；社保就业支出-4.8%，前值 2.6%；卫生健康支出同比-2.9%，较前值
（3.8%）回落；文化娱乐体育传媒支出增速 1.6%，由负转正（前值-9.6%）。基建类支出同比增速-1.3%，
前值-3.7%，拖累支出 0.3个百分点。其中交通运输支出同比-10.8%，前值-5.8%，降幅扩大；城乡社区事

务支出增速由前值-3.8%转正至 7.9%；农林水事务支出同比-0.2%，较前值（1.1%）小幅回落；环保支出-13.7%，
降幅继续收窄（前值-15.8%）。此外，债务付息支出同比-1.2%，较前值（7.0%）转负；科学技术支出同
比 6.9%，较前值（9.0%）回落，仍维持较快增长。

 政府性基金预算：收入降幅再度扩大、支出降幅收窄
9 月政府性基金收入同比-15.9%（前值-3.8%），降幅再度扩大。土地出让收入（-23.2%）拖累重新加重，
同样较前值（-12.6%）明显扩大。政府性基金支出同比-12.3%，降幅较前值（-21.4%）明显收窄，主要是
除土地出让收入相关支出外的其他支出降幅由-18.0%收窄至-8.4%，反映专项债、特别国债资金拨付有所
加快。
1-9月，第二本账收入累计增速为-18.6%，支出增速为-16.2%。其中土地出让收入 16087 亿，同比-27.9%，
降幅较 1-8月（-28.6%）收窄，但仍处深度负增长区间，按当前降幅外推，全年仍不及 3 万亿。后续来看，

随着专项债、超长期特别国债资金拨付加快与新型政策性金融工具持续投放，第二本账支出端有望延续修
复。

### Page 5

图13：政府性基金收支增速 图14：土地使用权出让收入与支出增速
资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理 资料来源:Wind，国信证券经济研究所整理
 总结：财政力度指数继续回升
财政力度指数连续第二个月回升。一方面是收入转负的同时支出降幅收窄。9月广义支出增速-3.7%，较前
值-6.7%明显收窄；广义收入当月同比增速-3.5%，前值 2.9%，由正转负。1-9月，广义财政支出累计增速
为-3.5%，完成度 65.2%，低于去年同期（67.1%）；广义财政收入累计增速为 1.4%，完成度 70.8%。另一
方面是赤字端同比收缩幅度放缓。1-9 月累计的广义财政缺口为 7.6万亿，较 2025年同期（8.8万亿）减
少约 1.3 万亿，同比收缩 14.4%，收缩幅度较 1-8 月（17.7%）有所收窄。此外，9 月新增财政存款-4300
亿，季末支出加快带动财政存款季节性回落，月末财政存款余额 7.9万亿；当月社融口径政府债券净融资
1.54万亿，较前值（1.01 万亿）明显回升。9月财政力度指数回升，连续第二个月回升。

后续来看，财政收支的 K 型分化将继续收敛。9月广义财政收入与支出增速已开始相向而行，政府债净融
资明显回升，8000 亿新型政策性金融工具持续投放，四季度专项债和超长期特别国债剩余额度发行使用仍
在途中。预计广义财政支出增速有望继续回升，关注政府债资金使用节奏与实物工作量形成情况。此外，
四季度只要增速达到 4.2%以上，全年经济增速就能完成 4.5%的目标，出台增量政策的必要性并不算太强，
现在的问题并非缺钱而是落地端。

2026 年獲利亮眼，惟 2027 年供給壓力漸增 
 歐美航線運價優於預期，預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.62 元：3Q26 營收
1,467.76 億元(+51.4%Y oY)，優於預期。受惠運價上漲獲利率提升，
然考量燃油成本占整體營業成本逾 2 成，油價上漲仍對成本形成壓力，
預估稅後淨利 424.74 億元(+95.3%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 19.62 元。 
 受地緣政治及供應鏈重組影響，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 13.24 元：展
望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug -26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增
4.6%，需求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年
逐步收斂。預估 2026 年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)，稅後淨利
991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 45.8 元。展望 2027 年，隨新船
陸續交付，儘管塞港情況仍可能限縮有效 運力，惟考量整體供給成長
仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步回歸常態。整體而言，預估2027 年
營收 4,097.3 億元(-13.5%YoY)，稅後淨利 482.4 億元(-51.4%Y oY)，
稅後 EPS 22.28 元。 
 維持中立評等，目標價 265 元：評價方面，以 2Q27F BVPS 元計算，
目前 PB 0.8X ，介於長榮疫情前區間 0.5-1.5X 中下緣，儘符合高股息
題 材 ， 然 隨 歐 美 傳 統 淡 季 到 來 ， 維 持 中 立 評 等 ， 目 標 價265 元
(2Q27BVPS PB 0.9X)。

個股報告 
長榮 為 全 球 貨 櫃 運 力 第七大 航
商，市占率 5.9% 
 長榮成立於 1968 年 9 月，實收資本額 216.5 億元。截至 3Q26，全球超過 1.3
萬位員工及 293 個代理行，航線擴及 101 個國家，總船隊數共 242 艘(自有船
比例超過 6 成)，預計至 2030 年仍有 72 艘船待交付。根據 Alphaliner 統計，
截至目前長榮為全球貨櫃運力第七大航商，市佔率約 5.9%。長榮 1H26 營收
結構為貨運收入 93%，船艙租收入 1.2%，其他 5.8%。

歐 美 航 線 運 價 優 於 預 期 ， 預 估
3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.62 元 
 長榮 Sep-26 營收 497.11 億元(+1.48%MoM，+63.75%Y oY)，3Q26 營收達成
率 105.5%，優於預期，主要受惠歐美航線運價走升，且旺季附加費及燃油附
加費挹注營收 。獲利方面 ，受惠運價上漲帶動獲利率提升 ， 惟中東衝突於Jul-26
一度趨緩後再度升溫，且考量燃油成本占整體營業成本逾 2 成，油價上漲仍對
成本形成壓力，預估 3Q26 毛利率 38.91%。由於營收規模擴大，費用率有望
下降 ，預估營益率 35.71%，假設 匯率 持穩 ，預估稅後淨利 424.74 億元
(+164.9%QoQ，+95.3%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 19.62 元。

個股報告 
歐美航線需求放緩 ，預估 4Q26
稅後 EPS 14.89 元 
 根據上海航交所 10/9 公告，上海出口集裝箱運價指數 (SCFI) 3,664.11 點
(+0.05%WoW，+215.76%Y oY)，隨中國十一長假結束，歐線運價短期反彈，
分別為遠東-歐洲線+3.41%WoW，遠東-地中海線+0.2%WoW；美線持平，遠
東-美西線+0.08%WoW，遠東-美東-0.04%WoW。觀察 10/11 公告跨大西洋、
歐 洲 及 跨 太 平 洋 航 線 運 力 分 別 變 動 -1.14%WoW 、 -1.88%WoW 及
+0.48%WoW。整體而言，隨歐美航線逐步進入傳統淡季，航商陸續調整運力
配置，其中歐洲線運力投放減少，美線則大致持平，顯示航商持續透過運力調
節支撐市場運價。

展望 4Q26，需求面而言，歐線隨傳統旺季結束，終端需求動能轉弱，預期後
續運價將呈緩步回落走勢。美線方面，旺季需求已近尾聲，且部分船班裝載率
已有下滑，儘管 Sep-26 川習會後美國調降部分消費品關稅，包括玩具、小型
家電及節慶裝飾品等，惟目前尚未出現明顯大型備貨潮。供給面而言，隨水情
陸續改善 ， 巴拿馬運河管理局預計自2026/10/15 起將每日允許通行量由 32 艘
回升至 33 艘，惟仍低於原先 36 艘水準。考量大型貨櫃船通行具一定優先性，
目前對船期營運影響相對有限，惟通行量尚未完全恢復，仍可能使美東線有效
運力有所收斂。另，美伊衝突及紅海通行風險仍存， Gemini 聯盟持續恢復部
分紅海航線，近期亦傳出 Premier Alliance 有望於 4Q26 恢復部分紅海航線，
若後續其他航商陸續跟進，繞航所吸收之有效運力恐逐步釋放，對市場運價形
成壓力。

整體而言，儘管近洋線進入傳統旺季，惟長榮歐美航線營收占比較高，預期整
體運價及貨量將隨淡季到來而回落 ， 預估4Q26 營收 1,350.87 億元(-8%QoQ，
+57.6%Y oY)。獲利方面，儘管油價仍維持高位，惟隨貨量減少，相關變動成
本亦有望下降，預估毛利率 37.9%。另考量長榮往年於 4Q 提列年終獎金費
用，預期費用率將季增，預估營益率 29.4%。假設匯率持穩，預估稅後淨利
323.53 億元(-23.8%QoQ，+279.8%Y oY)，4Q26 稅後 EPS 14.94 元。

2026 年有效運力供給受限 ， 預估
稅後 EPS 45.75 元 
 展望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug-26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增 4.6%，需
求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年逐步收斂。短期而
言，受到缺工及天候因素影響，部分港口作業效率下降，近期塞港現象增加，
有助吸收部分有效運力。展望 2027 年，在尚未考慮紅海全面復航之前提下，
Clarksons 預估全球貨櫃供給+8.8%Y oY，需求+3.5%YoY，供需缺口將再度擴
大；若紅海航線進一步恢復，繞航所吸收之有效運力亦將逐步釋放，供給壓力
恐進一步增加。需求方面，則須持續關注停戰協議進展、美國關稅政策變化及
通膨壓力對終端需求之影響。

展望 2026 年，長榮持續優化航線布局，強化東南亞、中東、非洲及中南美洲
等區域配置 ， 並透過長約布局降低市場運價波動影響，整體而言 ，元富預估 2026
年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)。獲利方面，預估毛利率 30.8%，營益率
25.4%，稅後淨利 991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 45.8 元。
展望 2027 年，隨新船陸續交付，全球貨櫃船隊運力持續增加，儘管塞港情況
仍可能限縮有效運力，惟考量整體供給成長仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步
回歸常態。整體而言，預估 2027 年營收 4,097.3 億元(-13.5%Y oY)，稅後淨利
482.4 億元(-51.4%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 22.28 元。

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

Stronger revenue momentum while GM guidance came in softer 
TSMC hosted its 3Q26 analyst meeting on October 15. While we 
view the higher full-year 2026 revenue guidance and the further 
acceleration in capacity buildout as the key positives, the GM 
guidance could be the softer spot. In the meeting, management 
further raised its 2026 revenue growth outlook to grow by around 
44% YoY (from slightly above 40%; USD term), supported by 
continued strong demand especially for AI/HPC. However, both 
3Q26 GM result and 4Q26 GM guidance came in lower than our 
expectations; we believe this is to reflect a steeper N2 ramp and 
overseas fab dilution in the near term. 
AI demand still ahead of supply, with no sign of slowdown 
Key questions among investors largely surrounded demand outlook. 
Overall, we think that management’s tone on AI demand remained 
very bullish. While TSMC did not formally update its long-term AI 
accelerator revenue CAGR, management indicated the CAGR will 
probably be higher, noting demand is extremely robust with no sign 
of slowdown and still far exceeds supply, with multiple drivers 
reinforcing this view, including - 1) agentic AI driving incremental 
CPU demand, which is excluded from TSMC’s AI accelerator 
definition and implies upside beyond the headline CAGR; 2) broader 
adoption of open-source models lowering the barrier to AI usage 
and lifting leading-edge silicon demand; 3) continued demand for 
networking chips alongside AI accelerators; and 4) TSMC’s own 
checks on US data center build progress and power supply, where 
management sees CSPs as very well prepared.  
Capacity build-out accelerating again: N2 capacity more than 
double in 2027; additional N3 fab addition; Texas under evaluation 
On N2, management for the first time guided its N2 capacity will be

### Page 3

more than double in 2027. As for N3, management announced an additional 3nm fab
expansion in Tainan on strong customer demand, with volume production in 1H28. This
comes on top of 1) the ongoing N3 fab expansion in Tainan (ramp in 1H27), 2) Arizona
fab 2 (ramp in 3Q27, slightly ahead of its prior schedule), 3) Japan fab 2 (ramp in 2028),
and 4) continued N5-to-N3 tool conversion. Beyond Arizona, TSMC confirmed it is
evaluating Texas as a potential additional US site, and is in discussion with one customer

building Terafab on utilizing that capacity; while the business model and structure
remains under discussion, TSMC highlighted that its principle is to run the fab by itself.
GM: steeper N2 dilution near term, but “no ceiling” long term
3Q26 GM declined 1.3ppt QoQ to 66.4%, primarily on dilution from the initial N2 ramp,
and 4Q26 GM is guided down a further 40bps to 66.0% at the mid-point on continued
N2 dilution and overseas fabs. Management now expects overseas fab dilution to be

closer to 2% for full-year 2026 (vs. 2-3% previously), helped by larger scale and cost
improvement, but reiterated it will widen to 3-4% in the latter part of the five-year
period as more US fabs ramp. Longer term, management stated there is “no ceiling” for
TSMC’s profitability, though any dramatic increase will not happen overnight. On pricing,
management reiterated a strategic approach by segment, location and technology, with
US production priced differently from Taiwan. Overall, we slightly lower our GM to

66.6%/66.8%/67.7% in 2026-2028E (vs. 67.2%/67.5%/67.8% previously).
CapEx: 2026 unchanged, multi-year step-up reiterated
2026 CapEx guidance was unchanged at US$60-64bn. While management did not
provide numeric guidance beyond 2026, but reiterated that 2026-2028 CapEx will be
significantly higher than the prior three years, and that revenue growth should continue
to outpace CapEx growth. Management acknowledged the tools, material and labor
inflation, but said the majority of CapEx is driven by long-term demand. Net net, we

maintain our 2026E/2027E/2028E CapEx at US$64bn/US$85bn/US$98bn.
High confidence in its structural lead
Addressing questions regarding rising competition, management remains confident in its
technology lead  for both front end wafer and advanced packaging technology. Despite
the later adoption timeframe (planned for in 2030) vs peers on High NA EUV, TSMC does
not see the later adoption than peers as a disadvantage. In advanced packaging, TSMC is
in volume production at 5x reticle size, which management believes no peer can match

today, with 9x and 14x in development and CoPoS for future cost reduction. In addition,
management also highlighted that it expects A14 (volume production in 2028) to be a
bigger node than N2, with customer engagement already exceeding N2’s at the same
stage.

### Page 5

3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance recap

4Q26 guidance: revenue well above expectations, GM below GSe 
For 4Q26, management guided revenue to US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the 
mid-point; based on FX [TWD/USD] of 31.8), GM to 65-67% and OpM to 56.5-58.5%. 
Revenue guidance came in well above GSe of +11.0% QoQ, supported by continued 
strong leading-edge demand including the steep N2 ramp. However, the GM mid-point 
of 66.0% is 1.3ppt below GSe of 67.3% and broadly in line with consensus of 66.1%, as 
continued N2 dilution and overseas fabs are only partly offset by productivity gains and 
cross-node capacity optimization. 
3Q26 results: revenue and EPS beat, GM below GSe 
3Q26 revenue of NT$1,494.2bn (US$46.72bn) was up 17.6% QoQ and 50.9% YoY, above 
the high end of guidance in USD terms and above GSe/consensus by 0.8%/2.1%. EPS of 
NT$29.43 was up 8.0% QoQ and 68.7% YoY, above GSe/consensus by 1.2%/3.7%; 
excluding the NT$2.24 one-off gain from the VIS disposal in 2Q26, EPS grew 18% QoQ. 
GM of 66.4% (-1.3ppt QoQ) landed in the upper half of guidance (65-67%) but 1.1ppt 
below GSe, while OpM of 58.7% beat the high end of guidance (56-58%) on stable OpEx 
at 7.8% of revenue. 
By technology, N2 contributed 8% of wafer revenue (vs. 3% in 2Q26) and N3 33% (vs. 
30%), taking advanced nodes (7nm and below) to 79% (vs. 77%). By platform, HPC rose 
22% QoQ to 68% of revenue (vs. 66%), with smartphone +10% QoQ at 21%. Inventory 
days fell 5 days QoQ to 82 days on higher wafer shipments.

### Page 7

Reiterate Buy; 12m TP maintain at NT$3,300 
We maintain our 12m TP at NT$3,300  based on a target P/E of 22x (unchanged, 
benchmarked to 1.0stdv above 5-year avg. forward P/E) applied to our 2027E EPS. 
Accordingly, we maintain our 12 month TP for the ADR at US$660, based on an 
unchanged FX conversion of USD/TWD of 30.0 and unchanged ADR premium of 20%. 
Our 22x target P/E multiple is also benchmarked to TSMC’s trading average of 22x 
during its previous revenue upcycle where its 3-year revenue CAGR (2019-2021) was 
28%, vs. our 3-year revenue CAGR at 38% during 2025-2028E. 
Our TP implies 35% (ADR 54%) upside to the last close. As a result, we reiterate our Buy 
ratings for TSMC/ADR. We maintain our constructive view on TSMC’s long-term growth 
outlook as we believe the company’s solid technology leadership and execution better 
positions it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural growth 
opportunities, especially in AI.

### Page 9

Investment Thesis, Price Target Risks and Methodology

Investment Thesis - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
TSMC is a leading global foundry company specializing in cutting-edge nodes. Its leading 
technology stance enables it to enjoy more than 60% revenue share in the global 
foundry market. We like TSMC as we believe its solid technology leadership and 
execution better position it vs. peers to capture the industry’s long-term structural 
growth opportunities, particularly in areas such as AI/5G/HPC/EV. In addition, valuation 
looks attractive in our view, with the shares trading at the mid-range of their 10-year 
trading history (P/E: 10-29x). Furthermore, we view TSMC as the key AI enabler among 
our Taiwan Semis coverage, thanks to its leadership stance in leading edge nodes and 
advanced packaging technology – CoWoS (chip on wafer on substrate). We believe TSMC 
will achieve its 25% revenue CAGR target for the next several years, driven primarily by 
increasing silicon content growth and AI/HPC demand, with LT GM to remain at 56%+. 
We are Buy rated.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - TSMC (2330.TW/TSM) 
Valuation methodology: We have a 12m TP of NT$3,300, which is derived by applying a 
target P/E multiple of 22x to our 2027E EPS. Our 22x target P/E is benchmarked against 
1.0stdv above its 5-year trading average. For the ADR (TSM), we have a 12m TP of 
US$660, based on a USD/TWD rate of 30.0 and an ADR premium of 20%. 
Key downside risks to our views: (1) further deterioration in end-demand recovery 
impacting capacity utilization; (2) slower customer node migrations; (3) slowdown in AI 
investment resulting in lower long-term semiconductor content growth; (4) poor 
yields/execution resulting in worse-than-expected profitability; (5) stronger 
competition resulting in ASP/profitability erosion; and (6) unfavorable FX trend or 
higher-than-expected cost increase weighing on the margin outlook.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total

19 October 2026 | 3:24AM HKT
3-month revenues preview: We expect AVC’s monthly revenues to continue 
sequential growth through Dec 2026, leading 4Q26 revenues to grow at +42% YoY/ 
+15% QoQ, driven by: (1) new AI server racks ramp-up, driving the overall liquid 
cooling penetration rate. As we highlighted in Oct (report link), we expect AI server 
rack (NVL72-equivalent) shipment (incl. Nvidia and AMD) to reach 63k/ 110k/ 164k 
in 2026-28E, reflecting our positive view on AI server racks. (2) Its major ASIC AI 
server customer’s new model ramp-up, driving its air-cooling and liquid-cooling 
components, along with a rising liquid cooling penetration rate. (3) Its customer base 
expansion to a new ASIC AI server, to further drive its liquid cooling components in 
4Q26. And (4) increasing adoption rate of ultra-thin VC in consumer electronics to fit 
the lightweight design and major customer’s foldable phone model. We expect AVC’s 
net income to grow at a +39% CAGR in 2026-28E, riding on the strong AI spending, a 
growing liquid cooling penetration rate, and AVC’s leading market position in total 
solutions (air cooling, liquid cooling with various components / systems). Maintain 
Buy.

Earnings revisions: We factor in AVC’s Sep revenue and revise down our 2026E net 
income by -1%, mainly on lower revenues in 4Q26E, reflecting the slow consumer 
electronics under high memory costs and AI servers model transition. We revise up our 
2027-28E net income by 4% / 0%, mainly on higher revenues and GMs. Our 2027-28E 
revenue is revised up by 1% / 1%, mainly on a higher contribution from AI server liquid 
cooling components. 2027-28E GM are revised up to reflect the rising liquid cooling 
components driving profitability.

Valuation: We continue to use a near-term P/E to derive our 12-month target price and 
update our target P/E multiple to 30.6x 2027E EPS (vs. 30.3x previously), which is still 
derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E multiple correlation. Our target P/E 
multiple is above AVC’s historical average +1stv. forward trading P/E, reflecting our 
positive view on AVC riding on the rising penetration rate of liquid-cooled AI servers. 
With our updated target P/E and earnings estimates, we raise our 12-month target price 
to NT$4,736 (from NT$4,513). Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology - AVC 
We are Buy-rated on AVC. Our 12-month target price for AVC is based on a near-term 
P/E, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our target price of 
NT$4,736 on a target P/E multiple of 30.6x applied to our forward-year EPS (2027E). 
Our target P/E is derived from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers 
in technology hardware.  
Key downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected adoption of liquid cooling; (2) 
lower-than-expected demand for AI servers; (3) fiercer-than-expected competition; 
and (4) a slower-than-expected recovery in general servers.

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

M
Idea
Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
2H26 Driven by Semis and
Photonics; 2027 Seen as
Another Strong Year
A unique combination of power, electrical and optical testing
tool capabilities position the company well in AI deployment.
Stay OW.
We see 3Q26 EPS of NT$13.71: Chroma's 3Q26 revenue came in at NT$17,926mn,
up 32% QoQ and 180% YoY. We believe the strength was driven mainly by the
semiconductor and photonics businesses, while the power testing business likely

remained at an elevated level. Within the semiconductor and photonics businesses,
SL T  should benefit from AI HPC customers' ramping; meanwhile, optical testing
tools for transceivers and, to a lesser extent, CPO, are also seen as growth drivers
during the quarter. We estimate GM of 60.5% (flat QoQ) as scale tailwinds should
be largely offset by a less favorable product mix, while OPM should expand by
0.5ppt QoQ to 39.3% on some operating leverage. Overall, we expect EPS of NT

$13.71 (+13% QoQ/+15% YoY).
Growth momentum seen sustained into 2027: Looking ahead, we expect Chroma
to see another year of strong growth, supported by the power testing (51% growth
for 42% mix) and semi & photonics businesses (58% growth for 40% mix).
• Power testing: The ever-increasing power consumption of AI clusters should
continue to drive demand for higher spec testing tools. Along with our
forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit demand in the next

three years (see report), we think this should stay a major driver for Chroma.
• SLT: We are seeing more HPC chip designers adopting the system level
testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. As the first mover in this market. Chroma should benefit from
increasing chip volume as well as higher TDP, in our view.
• Photonics: Transceivers should continue to be the mainstream solution;
architectures such as CPO and NPO also need testing tools.

• Emerging business: We expect Chroma, by leveraging its existing customer
relationship and technology expertise, to gain more traction in FT handlers
and burn-in ovens for AI HPCs in 2027. These are the markets that Chroma
previously focused less on, so they represent new opportunities.
Stay OW: We remain constructive on Chroma for its testing equipment's unique
combination of power, electrical and optical capabilities, allowing it to capture
growth opportunities in various aspects of AI deployment. Our PT remains NT

$2,800 as our estimate increases are offset by a lower target multiple of 40x 2027e
P/E (previously 45x) to factor in generally more conservative sentiment toward the
AI space now. This implies a PEG of 0.6 (vs. 1.0 over the past decade).

A Closer Look
Test instrument and ATS segment set to reach another
record high in 2026
Revenue for the segment increased 55% YoY to NT$10,546mn (37% of the mix) in 2025.
We estimate it will rise 118% YoY to NT$24,564mn (40% of the mix) in 2026. The ever-
increasing power consumption of AI clusters should continue to drive demand for higher
spec testing tools. Along with our forecast of a 75% CAGR for global power supply unit

demand in the coming three years (see our report), this should remain a major driver for
Chroma, in our view.

Multiple drivers in the semiconductor business
System-level testing  (SL T) business
SL T should benefit from the capex cycle of NVIDIA's Rubin generation GPU, AMD's MI450
GPU and more ASIC accelerators.  Management pointed out that discrete testing tools are
still preferred  by major customers.  We are also seeing more HPC chip designers adopting

the system level testing process to make sure defects won't be incorporated into the AI
systems. We believe Chroma, as the first mover in this market, should benefit from
increasing chip volume, as well as higher TDP.
Metrology business
The metrology business should continue to benefit from current advanced packaging
architectures from its foundry and OSAT customers. It is confident that the performance
of its tools can win future architecture projects, which our industry checks suggest should

have higher metrology density.

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Exhibit 3: Chroma –  Offerings for Metrology in Advanced Packaging
Source: Chroma
Chroma's metrology equipment employs white light interferometry technology. The basic
working principle is that white light is emitted on the reference mirror and the sample,
which are placed at the same distance from the light source. The reflected beams from
the reference mirror and sample surface are then received by a light sensor.
Interference occurs when two light beams collide, which could cause  them to either

strengthen or weaken, depending on the phase of the two light beams. Thus, by
measuring the intensity of the combined light beams, the distance between light sensor
and multiple points on the target surface can be calculated through an algorithm. This can
then be used to measure the dimensions of the structure or the alignment of layers.
Exhibit 4: Concept of Light Interference
Source: Keyence
Exhibit 5: Setup of Interferometer
Source: Wikipedia
CPO contribution to start from 2026

Chroma has been qualified for optical engine testing in Insertion 3 and system-level
testing in Insertion 4, and there might be incremental opportunities if supply chain players'
progress falls behind for testing equipment in Insertion 2. Over the longer term, Chroma
also  aspires to participate in the PIC optical/electrical/reliability testing in Insertion 1.

FT handlers to add another revenue stream
In HPC testing, Chroma used to focus mostly on the SL T side, although it did unveil its first
HPC FT handler earlier this year and is now going through the qualification process. We
believe this is a natural extension of its expertise and customer relationships accumulated
in SL Ts. We expect Chroma to gain more traction in FT handlers for AI HPCs in 2027.

### Page 10

10
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Our price target (base case scenario value) remains  NT$2,800: Our PT is based on a
target multiple of 40x 2027e P/E (vs. 45x 2027e previously).   We lowered our multiple to
factor in recent volatility in market sentiment toward AI.   We view our multiple as justified
based on our estimated 68% operating profit CAGR over 2025-28. This implies a PEG of
0.6 vs. its average of 1.0 in the past decade, when the stock was trading at an average of

~19x forward P/E with an operating profit CAGR of 20%.
Bull case scenario value – NT$3,900 (unchanged): We apply a 55x multiple (vs. 62x P/E
previously)  to our earnings estimate for 2027.  This scenario reflects stronger  5G and HPC
demand, driving more earnings upside.
Bear case scenario value – NT$1,100 (unchanged): We apply a 16x P/E multiple (vs. 18x
P/E previously) to our earnings estimate for 2027. This scenario factors in weaker HPC
revenue as hyperscalers start to focus more on ROI for AI capex than they did in the past

couple of years.
Risks:
• Softer-than-expected AI capex
• Slower progress in advanced packaging capacity expansion
• Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Powertech Technology

Up-cycle level of earnings to continue; 
reiterate Buy

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 500.00 TWD | Price: 254.50 TWD

Reiterate Buy on above-trend growth and margins 
We believe Powertech Technology Inc. (PTI) can continue to benefit from the AI boom 
and an upturn in the memory chip industry. 3Q results clearly confirm record-high 
revenue (NT$25bn; up 27% YoY) and an above-trend gross margin (24%; normal high 
teen% or 20% range). 4Q guidance (for packaging/testing businesses) also remained 
bullish: DRAM – tight supply vs strong demand; NAND – order increase for more value-
added NAND and enterprise SSD; and logic – mix improvement with FC-BGA and price 
hike (following cost increase). Key customers are Micron, Kioxia, Solidigm (owned by SK 
Hynix) and various Greater China logic chip makers that require outsourced packaging 
and testing services. As we have already assumed up-cycle earnings level for 2027-28, 
our EPS estimates remain largely unchanged. We maintain our PO of NT$500 (27x 2027-
28E P/E applied). Reiterate Buy.  
Key takeaways from 3Q results  
Five takeaways from 3Q results: (1) strong execution in newly growing packaging orders 
for high-end memory (low-power / high-speed DRAM, enterprise SSD, 200+ layers 
NAND), (2) increasing exposure to more advanced logic (through FC-BGA, etc), (3) a well-
balanced sales mix (DRAM: 20% of total sales; NAND: 28%; SiP/modules 9%; logic 43%), 
(4) notably improved gross margins (limited increase in manufacturing costs vs strong 
top-line growth seen), and (5) solid FCF generation (EBITDA: c.NT$8bn vs capex: 
NT$5bn; expected annual dividend yield: 3-4% range).

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 15.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Headquartered in Taiwan, PTI is one of the largest OSAT 
firms, which packages and tests memory and logic chips. It 
has around 18,000 employees. The logic chip-related 
business operates under Greatek Electronics (fully 
consolidated into earnings). Management's new focus areas 
in packaging/testing are PLP, TSV, 3D NAND, low-power 
DRAM, SSD, etc.

Investment Rationale 
Our Buy rating is based on strong earnings growth, driven 
by more value-added memory and even logic packaging 
solutions. 1Q-3Q26 earnings have already hit upcycle levels, 
posting a strong YoY growth (largely driven by NAND/SSD). 
We believe the memory super-cycle and customers' 
ongoing outsourcing trend (Micron, Solidigm/Hynix, Kioxia 
and Greater China's logic chipmakers) using Powertech's 
proven backend solutions should be the key contributors to 
strong earnings growth by 2028.

Exhibit 1: Powertech is accelerating its advanced FOPLP packaging strategy through significant capacity expansion, strategic partnerships (including 
Broadcom), and customer wins such as AMD, positioning itself as a key beneficiary of growing AI semiconductor packaging demand from 2027 onward 
Powertech’s recent news summary during Jul-Aug 2026 
Date Key news Summary 
Aug-2026 FOPLP 
production 
-Powertech plans to start production at the world's first panel-level chip packaging facility for AI chips as soon as early 2027 to make server CPUs, AI 
computing chips and advanced optics 
-Powertech said its advanced FOPLP production line has secured a 100% UTR through 2030 ahead of its scheduled volume productio n in 2027 
-Powertech plans to invest NT$70bn (US$2.2bn) in its FOPLP facility, with installed capacity set to increase from 1,000 panels per months in 2026 
to 5,000 in 2028 
Jul-2026 JV with Broadcom -Powertech reported plans to invest US$400mn to create a new JV with Broadcom in Singapore to cater to customer demand for advanced panel-
based packaging services 
-The JV will concentrate on offering additive finer-geometry redistribution layer (RDL) tech for advanced package substrates  
 AMD business -Powertech reaffirmed that it is on track to start shipping AI chips utilizing its advanced PLP tech for AMD in 2H27, dismissing concern s over delays 
-AMD will be one of Powertech's first customers adopting the FOPLP tech, which is considered to be more cost-effective than the mainstream 
CoWoS tech 
  Capex/capacity -Powertech plans to acquire the entire Philippine unit of ON Semiconductor for US$45mn as part of its efforts to navigate global supply chain 
realignment amid escalating geopolitical risks 
-The transaction will be conducted by its subsidiary, Greatek Electronics in which Powertech holds 43% stake

### Page 13

Price objective basis & risk 
Powertech Technology (PWOGF) 
Our PO of NT$500 is derived from 27x P/E based on 2027-28E EPS. This is 10-20% 
higher than 2024-25 peak levels (low- to mid-20x). Since we expect 50%+ higher EPS in 
2026-28E vs 2024-25 on the back of record-high sales/margin, we believe fair multiples 
should be at least on par with the previous peak level. True, the long-term historical 
average P/E came in the range of 10-20x, but the memory super-cycle and AI boom 
should warrant above-trend earnings growth and consequently over 20x fair P/E. 
Taiwan's OSAT stocks also trade at 20-30x P/E.

Upside risk is mostly related to strong packaging orders from memory chipmakers and 
even logic chip suppliers.

Downside risk is mostly associated with an unexpected memory industry downturn or AI 
bubble (e.g., significant packaging order cut).

結論: 
力成法說會大致符合預期，其中: (1) 3Q26 毛利率略優於預期，稅後
EPS 符合預期 。(2) 4Q26 營收穩健成長 ，UTR 可望持續提升 。(3) FOPLP
缺機台情況有明顯改善，擴產時程不受影響。(4) 2027 年營運將由
FOPLP、FCBGA 等先進封裝驅動成長。由於營運展望與上季變化不大，
大致維持 2026、2027 年獲利預估，預估稅後 EPS 分別為 12.89 元、
18.57 元，考量力成策略佈局以先進封裝廠為定位，可望擺脫記憶體
景氣循環既定印象，故維持PER 20X，目標價 370 元，維持買進建議。

重點摘要: 
1.3Q26 毛利率略優於預期:  
力成 3Q26 營收+9.6%QoQ(+26.9%YoY)， 主要係AI 伺服器帶旺 eSSD 需
求成長 ， 且先進邏輯封裝 、 子公司超豐， 以及Tera Probe/Tera Power
均有雙位數QoQ 成長表現 。 在毛利率方面 ， 較前季提升2.2pt 至24.0%，
略優於預期的 23.5%，反映產品組合優化，且因應原物料成本提升進
行個位數~雙位數%不等的漲價。OPM 亦較前季提升 2.4pt 至 17.7%，
單季稅後 EPS 3.55 元，大致符合預期的 3.60 元。

2.4Q26 穩健成長，維持 2026 年獲利預估不變:  
展望 4Q26，預估營收持續穩定成長，預期 Dram 產能 UTR 維持高檔且
有價格效益，企業級 SSD 需求也將維持高檔，另 AI 相關應用將持續
扮演主要成長引擎，高階 FCBGA 已成功導入大晶片封裝並延續成長動
能，TPJ+TPW 因伺服器及 AI 強勁需求也將保持穩健，相較之下，超豐
在 AI、HPC 及 IoT 需求成長，但消費性電子 、PC 及 NB 拉貨轉趨保守。
目前整體封裝和測試 UTR 都超過 90%，4Q26 將隨營收增加而持續提
升。預估 2026 全年營收+27.9%YoY，稅後 EPS 12.89 元(原估 12.87
元)。

3. FOPLP 缺機台情況有明顯改善，擴產時程不受影響:  
在 FOPLP 進度方面，客戶端產品驗證持續推進，朝向 2027 量產既定
目標穩定推進，且隨著市場需求及量產信心度提升，客戶NPI 的需求
亦顯著增加。在設備交貨狀況方面，儘管半導體廠商對設備需求十分
吃緊，但 在公司與 供應商共同努力下，缺機台情況有明顯改善，
2027~2028 年擴產將按計畫進行，出貨時程不受影響。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

預計 AMD 的 AI 晶片將率先導入 FOPLP， 並於2027 年中以前如期量產，
預估第一階段產能 2K/月。

4.2027 年將由 FOPLP、FCBGA 等先進封裝驅動成長:  
展望 2027 年，力成營運成長將從過去高度仰賴記憶體產品，重塑為
以先進封裝包括 FOPLP、2.5D、3D、矽光 CPO 及高階 FCBGA 所驅動的
成長模式 。 目前先進封裝佔比30%， 預計2027 年在 FOPLP 設備問題改
善，且如期導入 AMD AI 產品下，先進封裝比重將持續提升。此外，
記憶體供需在 2027 年緩和但仍供不應求，在記憶體大廠產能陸續開
出下，有助於記憶體營收穩增並維持高稼動率。預估 2027 年營收
+22.1%YoY， 稅後淨利+45.8%YoY， 稅後EPS 18.57 元(原估 18.25 元)。

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사HD현대중공업(329180) 피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

(329180) 
HD 현대중공업 
2026.10.29

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

피크아웃 우려를 이겨낼 조선사 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 610,000원(하향) 
 현재주가 285,500원(10/28)

 3Q26 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2%yoy), 영업이익은 8,992억원
(+61.3%yoy, OPM 15.6%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센서스 9,792억원 하회.  
 평균 환율이 약 79원 하락하며 영업이익 약 450억원이 줄었고, 조업
일수는 47일로 통상 계절성 대비 4일 추가 감소해 약 500억원의 이
익 감소 효과 발생. 임단협 추가 비용의 약 75%도 3분기에 선반영
(잔여분은 4분기 반영). 일회성 요인을 제외하면  이익 체력은 견조. 
 동사의 조선 부문 매출액은 4조 3,988억원(+44.0%yoy), 영업이익은 
6,463억원(+62.8%yoy, OPM 14.7%)을 기록. 상선 OPM은 14.7%로 
2Q26(17.8%) 대비 하락했으나 대부분 외생변수 영향. 3Q26 대형선 매출 
중 2023년 수주분 비중은 45%, 2024~2025년 수주분은 51%. 고선가 
물량의 매출 비중이 높아지는 2027년까지 수익성 개선 여력은 충분.  
 9월 말 누적 상선 수주는 138.0억달러로 연간 목표의 120%, 엔진기계는 
41.4억달러로 155%를 달성했으나 함정(13%)과 해양(5%)은 부진. LNG선
은 중국 조선소의 저가 수주와 발주 둔화로 선가가 일부 하락. 선주들이 현 
선가를 저점으로 인식하며 발주 협의를 재개하는 분위기. 북미 LNG 프로젝
트 FID가 이어지고 있어 2027년 LNG선 발주 회복 기대. 
 함정 부문은 매출 2,570억원(-14.6%qoq)으로 줄었으나, 수출 비중 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선으로 영업이익 386억원(+114%qoq, OPM 15.0%)을 기록. 
해양플랜트는 매출액 3,799억원(+35.3%yoy), 영업이익 1,265억원(OPM 
33.3%). C/O 180억원을 제외해도 Ruya 공정 본격화와 Trion 고수익 물량
으로 높은 수익성 유지. 2027년 일부 프로젝트 인도로 매출 감소가 예상되
나, LNG선·블록 물량 투입으로 고정비 부담을 완화할 계획. 신규 해상변전
소(OSS) 계약을 앞두고 있으며, 2027년 초 추가 수주 발표도 기대. 
 주목할 부분은 엔진기계. 영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)으로 높은 D/F 
엔진 비중을 바탕으로 견조한 수익성 유지. 육상발전용 힘센엔진은 올해   
3GW를 확보하며 2029년까지 계획 캐파 수준의 물량을 이미 채움. 생산 
예상 물량은 2028년 약 1.5GW, 2029년 약 3GW이며, 신규 공장 구축 이
후 연간 생산능력은 약 4GW로 확대 예정. 북미 Hyperscaler와의 직접 협
상이 늘고 있으며, 힘센엔진을 활용한 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)로 FDC 시장
까지 대응 가능. AI 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2028년 이후 새로운 이익 축. 
 미국에서는 Kiewit과 합작법인 설립 계약 체결. 군수지원함 등 지원함 사업
부터 추진하며, 현지 조선소 건설 과정에서 HII에 선박 블록을 공급하는 방
안도 협의 중. 구체적인 투자계획은 2026년 말까지 확정 목표. 국내에서는 
핵추진잠수함 초기 예산이 2027년 반영되며 동사도 사업 참여를 준비 중. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 61만원을 제시. 고선가 물량 매출 확
대와 육상발전용 엔진 성장으로 피크아웃을 이겨낼 굴지의 조선사.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.

MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.
Investment Implications
In our view, any related market
weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and
capital investments.

THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Price Return is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States. A price return index is a stock
market index that tracks only the price changes and capital gains or losses of its component securities, without including dividends or cash distributions.
S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index is an early monthly economic indicator that estimates overall private-sector business activity by combining manufacturing

investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

RBNZ: +0.8%
Market median: +0.8%, range +0.5% to +1.2%

Annual change: +3.7% (prev: +4.1%)
Westpac forecast: +3.7%, RBNZ: +3.9%, Market: +3.8%

Non-tradables
Quarterly change: +1.0% (prev: +0.6%)
Westpac forecast: +1.3%
Annual change: +3.3% (prev: +3.4%)

Tradables
Quarterly change: +0.2% (prev: +2.7%)
Westpac forecast: -0.2%
Annual change: +4.3% (prev: +4.9%)

Consumer prices rose 0.7% in the September quarter. That saw the annual inflation rate easing to
3.7%, down from 4.1% in the year to June. 
Today’s result was in line with our forecast, but lower than the RBNZ’s forecast for 3.9% annual
inflation. 
However, under the surface, the details were more concerning. Measure of core inflation have taken a
step higher, with several closely watched measures now at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s
target band. 
We suspect that today’s result will leave the market continuing to price a strong chance of another
OCR hike at next Wednesday’s policy review. In that light, we think the RBNZ will regard this as the
path of least resistance, and we now expect that they will deliver (previously we expected that they
would pause and assess, before hiking at the December MPS).

What contributed to inflation in the September quarter?
Underpinning September’s 0.7% rise in consumer prices were some big price movements in both
directions. 
The largest move in prices this quarter was actually a sharp fall in fuel costs. Fuel prices aren’t low by
any stretch of the imagination. However, average prices for both petrol and diesel over the September
quarter were lower than in the June quarter when prices rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle East

### Page 3

conflict. In terms of specifics, the average price for 91 unleaded was 6.5% lower in September than in
the June quarter, while diesel prices were close to 16% lower. Those moves took 0.4ppts off inflation
this quarter. 
While prices at the pump weren’t as high as they were in the June quarter, fuel costs are still painfully
high. And that’s been bleeding through into higher prices for other goods and services. Of note, the
September quarter saw large increases in passenger transport costs, with international airfares up 14%
and domestic airfares up nearly 8%. 
The September quarter also saw the annual increases in local council rates (up 6.2%) and charges for
services like water supply (up 7.5%). That continues the run of large increases seen in recent years.
Lastly, the past few months saw a 1.0% rise in food prices, largely as a result of the seasonal rise in
prices for fresh vegetables. 
On the downside, we’ve seen continued softness in housing rents, which are the largest component of
the CPI, accounting for 11.2% of the basket. With low population growth and ample supply, rents were
flat in the September quarter and have effectively been flat since the start of this year. In annual terms,
rental inflation is the lowest it’s been in more than two decades, with regions like Wellington seeing
outright falls.

Annual and core inflation 
The annual inflation rate dropped back to 3.7% in the September quarter, down from 4.1% in the year
to June and the highest level in two years.
Looking under the surface, prices in the domestically oriented non-tradables group rose 1.0% over the
quarter, less than expected. That saw annual non-tradables inflation nudging down to 3.3% (from 3.4%
in the year to June). 
Non-tradables inflation has continued to run at above-average rates over the past year. That’s despite
the softness in economic growth and the labour market. In large part, that’s due to the continued
sizeable increases in administered prices, such as electricity charges which rose 9.3% over the past
year. 
However, prices in other areas have also been firm. Non-tradables excluding housing and utilities costs
(which also omits local council rates) was 3.4% for the year. In seasonally adjusted terms, quarterly
non-tradables inflation has been tracking around 0.8% for most of the past year. It was noted that in
sectors like construction, higher materials costs are adding to prices. Feedback from business we've
heard indicates that those higher materials costs are likely related to high oil prices. 
On the imported front, lower fuel costs saw tradable prices rising by just 0.2% over the September
quarter (though above our forecast for a small fall). That saw annual tradables inflation dropping back
to 4.3%, down sharply from the 4.9% rise in the year to June. 
While fuel costs have been a key driver for tradables inflation, under the surface, other imported
inflation pressures are looking firm. Excluding food and fuel costs, tradables prices were up 2.7% over
the past year. That’s the highest level in three years. 
With big swings in fuel costs, and concern that this is spilling over into operating costs more generally,
a key focus for the RBNZ is what’s been happening to measures of core inflation (which smooth
through quarter-to-quarter swings in individual prices and instead track the underlying trend in

### Page 4

Related articles
inflation). 
On this front we did some see worrying signs. Several closely watched measures of core inflation have
picked up, and now sit at the top of (or slightly above) the RBNZ target band. In terms of specifics, we
saw: 
Reinforcing this picture of firm underlying inflation pressures, a growing number of goods have been
increasing in price. That could be an early indication that businesses, even those with a limited
exposure to fuel costs, are starting to factor in expectations of continued high inflation when setting
prices.

Outlook 
While today’s result was lower than the RBNZ assumed in their September MPS projections, the
firmness in core inflation will give the RBNZ pause for thought ahead of their policy review on 28
October. Furthermore, with a sharp rise in petrol prices in recent weeks, September’s easing in annual
inflation is likely to be just a temporary reprieve. We expect headline inflation to reaccelerate back over
4% before the end of this year, and it’s set to remain above the RBNZ’s current forecast for most of
2027. 
Market pricing and views have coalesced around an October hike which we now suspect the MPC will
see as the path of least resistance. Core inflation measures are now close to or above 3%, which will
add fuel to arguments from the MPC hawks. We will have more detail in our MPR Preview later today,
including on the implications for the shape of the tightening cycle moving forward.

CPI ex-fuel, food and household energy: +2.9% yr (vs +2.5% previously)

Document 363: Industrials_ AI Jitters Versus Booming Orders

Source PDF: all report pdf/Industrials_ AI Jitters Versus Booming Orders.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Industrials: AI Jitters Versus Booming Orders
Date:    5 hours ago
Industrials: AI Jitters Versus Booming Orders

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Industrials: AI Jitters Versus
Booming Orders
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 28 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Ant Rozetsky / Unsplash

I. AI Still Boosting Capital Goods Orders
The AI investment boom has been a double-edged sword for S&P 500
Industrials this year. Back in July, we noted that the sector was the
second-best performer among the S&P 500's 11 sectors, with a 17.7% ytd
gain, as investors favored companies supplying the equipment and
infrastructure needed for the AI buildout. Since then, however, intensifying

### Page 2

AI jitters have weighed on industrial stocks, fueled by a potentially toxic
trifecta of financial constraints: elevated borrowing costs, uncertain
investment returns, and concerns about whether sufficient capital will be
available to finance everyone's ambitious AI spending plans.
The corresponding reversal has been significant. As of October 27,
Industrials was up just 8.2% ytd, compared with a 12.8% gain for the S&P
500, slipping to sixth place among the 11 sectors (chart).

The sector's stock price index is trading below its 200-day moving average
but remains on a solid multi-year uptrend (chart).
Performance within the sector has been uneven so far this year, with rail
transportation leading, while industrial machinery and aerospace and
defense lagging (chart).

### Page 3

We think the recent selloff is overdone and continue to recommend
overweighting Industrials. For starters, the sector's earnings outlook
remains upbeat. Forward earnings rose 13.6% ytd to a record high last
week (chart).
The sector's forward profit margin is also at a record high of 11.7% (chart).

### Page 4

Analysts' net revisions of their revenues and earnings estimates have also
improved significantly in recent months, with both remaining historically
elevated (chart).
Furthermore, analysts still expect double-digit earnings growth of 17.6% in
2026 and 16.5% in 2027 (chart).

### Page 5

The sector's forward P/E has fallen sharply recently, but remains above
the overall market's valuation multiple (chart).
The AI investment boom continues despite the stock market's jitters about
the AI trade. The five major hyperscalers (Amazon, Alphabet, Microsoft,
Meta, and Oracle) are projected to spend nearly $800 billion on capex this
year and more than $1 trillion in 2027. So far this week, there has been
roughly $235 billion in announced AI financing, including up to $95 billion

from Meta, a $40 billion chip-and-server leasing arrangement involving
SpaceX, and a potential $100 billion IPO by Anthropic. We therefore
continue to recommend overweighting Industrials, which should continue
to benefit from the buildout.

### Page 6

Indeed, the AI spending boom is still boosting industrial activity, with much
more in the pipeline. Consider the following:
(1) Durable goods. September durable goods orders rose 1.8% m/m, the
largest increase in five months. Orders excluding transportation increased
0.5%, while core capital goods orders (nondefense excluding aircraft), a
key gauge of business investment, rose 0.5% and 13.5% y/y to another
record high (charts).
The major components of durable goods orders are at record highs

(chart).

### Page 7

Orders for machinery necessary to operate data centers are especially
strong (chart).
Unfilled orders for computers and electronic products rose to a record
$162.4 billion in June (chart).

### Page 8

(2) Regional business surveys. All five regional Fed manufacturing
surveys are now available for October. The regional manufacturing PMI
edged down to 13.8 from September but remained comfortably in
expansion territory, suggesting that the national ISM M-PMI likely
remained above 50.0 in October (chart).
The average prices-paid index across regional Fed surveys stood at 57.0,
near levels last seen in 2022. Meanwhile, the prices-received index
climbed to 31.6, suggesting that manufacturers are increasingly passing

higher input costs on to customers (chart).

### Page 9

II. US Economy
The industrial sector isn't the only driver of the US economy showing
remarkable strength. Another key driver of economic resilience is
consumer spending. Here is more:
(1) Consumer spending. Redbook same-store sales rose 9.7% y/y
during the week of October 16. That is well above the 2025 average of
5.8% (chart).
(2) Labor market. Consumers' perceptions of labor-market conditions
improved in October. The percentage saying jobs were plentiful rose to

26.7%, the highest since April (chart).

### Page 10

The latest weekly ADP data show private-sector employment rising by an
average of 16,750 jobs per week over the four weeks through October 10,
or roughly 67,000 jobs on a monthly basis (chart). The labor market
remains in good shape.
(3) GDP. Ahead of Thursday's release of the Q3-2026 US GDP report, the
Atlanta Fed's final GDPNow estimate suggests that the economy
expanded at a robust 3.4% annualized rate during the quarter, supported
by strong consumer spending and business investment (chart).

### Page 11

clause.

Document 378: Leading Index still tracking a touch below trend (2026-10-29)
